```text
1	\section{Related Work}
2	
3	\paragraph{Execution Traces for Code LLMs.}
4	TracePile \citep{chen2025tracepile} scaled trace-augmented training to 2.6M samples; \citet{wang2025dcsh} found trace utility task- and format-dependent, which we extend by showing that complete traces degrade SFT performance while compact exception events suffice.
5	NExT \citep{ni2024next} and ET-CoT \citep{eguchi2025etcot} used trace chain-of-thought for repair and execution reasoning; \citet{ferrando2025execute} found compact traces comparable to full traces, supporting our density finding.
6	\citet{liu2023codeexecution} pre-trained on execution traces; CRUXEval \citep{gu2024cruxeval,xu2025cruxevalx} established execution reasoning benchmarks.
7	None decompose which trace components contribute to \emph{classification} accuracy.
8	
9	\paragraph{Code Verdict and Patch Classification.}
10	TRACED \citep{ding2024traced} showed execution-aware pretraining improves vulnerability detection; CC2Vec \citep{hoang2020cc2vec}, LLM4PatchCorrect \citep{zhou2024llm4patch}, and APPT \citep{appt2024} developed representation learning of code changes.
11	Verdict prediction is an instance of the test oracle problem \citep{barr2015oracle}; none isolate individual trace components.
12	
13	\paragraph{Fault Localization and Trace Selection.}
14	SBFL \citep{abreu2007accuracy,jones2002visualization,wong2014dstar} and LLM-based FL \citep{kang2024autofl,qin2024agentfl} share our insight that anomalous execution events carry the strongest diagnostic signal.
15	TraceCoder \citep{huang2026tracecoder} and InspectCoder \citep{wang2025inspectcoder} use selective trace extraction; RepairAgent \citep{bouzenia2025repairagent}, Agentless \citep{xia2024agentless}, SWE-agent \citep{yang2024sweagent}, and OpenHands \citep{wang2025openhands} incorporate execution feedback in repair workflows \citep{jimenez2024swebench}, yet which trace components carry verdict signals most efficiently remains unstudied.
16	Prompt compression \citep{jiang2023llmlingua,li2023selectivecontext} showed pruning low-information tokens preserves accuracy; our decomposition quantifies \emph{why}.
17	
18	\paragraph{Calibration, Data Selection, and Defect Prediction.}
19	Neural network calibration \citep{guo2017calibration}, extended to LLMs \citep{kadavath2022language,geng2024calibration,spiess2025calibration}, studies confidence-correctness alignment; our ECE ${<}$ 0.005 suggests compact inputs improve calibration.
20	The Information Bottleneck \citep{tishby2000information} predicts optimal representations compress task-irrelevant information; feature selection theory \citep{guyon2003introduction} shows irrelevant features degrade classifiers; LIMA \citep{zhou2023lima} demonstrates minimal high-quality data suffices for alignment.
21	Curriculum learning \citep{bengio2009curriculum}, dataset cartography \citep{swayamdipta2020cartography}, data selection \citep{sachdeva2024dataefficient,xie2023doremi}, and data pruning \citep{paul2021deep,sorscher2022beyond} show that removing low-value examples improves convergence, consistent with our finding that compact exceptions converge rapidly while verbose traces oscillate.
22	Just-in-time defect prediction \citep{kamei2013large} shares our fault detection motivation.
23	
```

</details>

```text
1	\section{Discussion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	
4	\paragraph{A descriptive two-pathway framework.}
5	The results suggest execution traces contain two independent signal pathways for verdict prediction, whose interaction explains why complete traces degrade SFT learning.
6	We present this as a descriptive working hypothesis, not a causal model.
7	
8	\paragraph{Explicit pathway: diagnostic tokens.}
9	Exception and assertion events directly encode fault occurrence.
10	The diagnostic token ratio $d$ (\S2.1) correlates with accuracy at $r{=}0.949$ ($p{=}0.014$) across five conditions, and all four models achieve ${\geq}$99.33\% under \texttt{exception\_only} with zero variance on Qwen ($\sigma{=}$0.00, 3 seeds).
11	We acknowledge that exception events structurally encode verdict information; the contribution is systematic quantification across four models and three families.
12	Although 55.5\% of failures are silent logic errors, \texttt{exception\_only} achieves 100\% fail recall because test assertions generate \texttt{AssertionError} events, confirming the pathway captures both crashes and assertion failures.
13	\texttt{ae\_only}, a strict superset, slightly underperforms \texttt{exception\_only} (Table~\ref{tab:ablation}), confirming that additional content introduces noise.
14	
15	\paragraph{Implicit pathway: terminal execution state.}
16	Tail-truncated traces achieve 93--97\% accuracy (Table~\ref{tab:tail_cross_model}) despite containing near-zero diagnostic tokens ($d {\approx} 0$).
17	The mechanism: whole-input tail truncation at ${\geq}$512 tokens captures \texttt{[Line~X]} variable values at final execution steps, which indirectly encode program outcome.
18	Below 384 tokens, the globals module dump consumes the entire budget, yielding ${\sim}$73\% ($\approx$ \texttt{no\_trace}).
19	The +13.8pp phase transition at tail-384$\to$tail-512 marks where the model crosses this noise floor and accesses terminal execution state.
20	This pathway is genuinely non-obvious: it operates on structured program state with zero explicit fault markers.
21	
22	\paragraph{Diagnostic signal dilution in complete traces.}
23	Both pathways' signals exist in full traces but are buried under thousands of intermediate tokens.
24	Figure~\ref{fig:training_dynamics} confirms: \texttt{exception\_only} converges within 50 steps, while \texttt{full\_trace} with label-only loss oscillates (82--89\%) without convergence (Appendix~\ref{sec:training_dynamics}); seed variance negatively correlates with diagnostic density.
25	
26	\begin{figure}[t]
27	\centering
28	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{training_dynamics}
29	\caption{Training dynamics: \texttt{exception\_only} converges to 99.83\% by step 150; \texttt{full\_trace} with label-only loss oscillates (82--89\%) without convergence.}
30	\label{fig:training_dynamics}
31	\end{figure}
32	
33	\paragraph{Why SFT over regex?}
34	SFT \texttt{exception\_only} (99.83\%) exceeds the best regex (B\_marker, 99.33\%) by 0.50pp on 6 caught-exception boundary cases (McNemar $p{=}.014$), provides calibrated probabilities (ECE\,=\,0.0027; \S\ref{sec:calibration}), and retains 97.65\% under marker removal vs.\ B5's 28.49\% (\S\ref{sec:nomarker}).
35	The residual 2/1190 errors (both \texttt{GeneratorExit}; Appendix~\ref{sec:irreducible}) are shared across seeds; CodeLlama-13B seed 123 achieves 100\%.
36	
37	\paragraph{The marginal value of complete traces.}
38	Source code alone achieves 73--76\% across four models (\texttt{no\_trace}), so the total incremental value of traces is at most ${\sim}$27pp.
39	Complete traces capture only 4--9pp of this headroom (77--82\%), while exception events capture 24--27pp (${\geq}$99.33\%).
40	The finding that more information degrades performance, rather than the success of compact signals, is the primary contribution: it challenges the common assumption that richer execution context benefits SFT.
41	This aligns with the classical principle that irrelevant features degrade classifiers \citep{guyon2003introduction}, the Information Bottleneck prediction that optimal representations compress task-irrelevant information \citep{tishby2000information}, and LIMA's \citep{zhou2023lima} demonstration that minimal high-quality data suffices for alignment.
42	
43	\paragraph{Design implications.}
44	The two-tier hierarchy (Table~\ref{tab:cross_model}) suggests a practical fallback: extract exception events first (explicit pathway); resort to tail-truncated execution state when no exception is raised (implicit pathway).
45	Tier~2 benefit is model-dependent (Qwen +10.9/+7.2pp at 7B/1.5B; CodeLlama +0.9pp), so trace design should match deployment model capacity.
46	Whether this framework transfers to other trace-based tasks \citep{gu2024cruxeval,ni2024next} remains open.
47	
48	\paragraph{Caveats.}
49	The decomposition (\S\ref{sec:density}) is a preliminary upper-bound estimate on the Qwen family only (CodeLlama and DeepSeek exhibit label-only collapse); $N{=}4$ models is insufficient for a continuous density--accuracy relationship.
50	HumanEval/MBPP covers only simple assert-based tests; real-world failure modes remain untested.
51	
```

    "originalFile": "\\section{Discussion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\n\\paragraph{A descriptive two-pathway framework.}\nThe results suggest execution traces contain two independent signal pathways for verdict prediction, whose interaction explains why complete traces degrade SFT learning.\nWe present this as a descriptive working hypothesis, not a causal model.\n\n\\paragraph{Explicit pathway: diagnostic tokens.}\nException and assertion events directly encode fault occurrence.\nThe diagnostic token ratio $d$ (\\S2.1) correlates with accuracy at $r{=}0.949$ ($p{=}0.014$) across five conditions, and all four models achieve ${\\geq}$99.33\\% under \\texttt{exception\\_only} with zero variance on Qwen ($\\sigma{=}$0.00, 3 seeds).\nWe acknowledge that exception events structurally encode verdict information; the contribution is systematic quantification across four models and three families.\nAlthough 55.5\\% of failures are silent logic errors, \\texttt{exception\\_only} achieves 100\\% fail recall because test assertions generate \\texttt{AssertionError} events, confirming the pathway captures both crashes and assertion failures.\n\\texttt{ae\\_only}, a strict superset, slightly underperforms \\texttt{exception\\_only} (Table~\\ref{tab:ablation}), confirming that additional content introduces noise.\n\n\\paragraph{Implicit pathway: terminal execution state.}\nTail-truncated traces achieve 93--97\\% accuracy (Table~\\ref{tab:tail_cross_model}) despite containing near-zero diagnostic tokens ($d {\\approx} 0$).\nThe mechanism: whole-input tail truncation at ${\\geq}$512 tokens captures \\texttt{[Line~X]} variable values at final execution steps, which indirectly encode program outcome.\nBelow 384 tokens, the globals module dump consumes the entire budget, yielding ${\\sim}$73\\% ($\\approx$ \\texttt{no\\_trace}).\nThe +13.8pp phase transition at tail-384$\\to$tail-512 marks where the model crosses this noise floor and accesses terminal execution state.\nThis pathway is genuinely non-obvious: it operates on structured program state with zero explicit fault markers.\n\n\\paragraph{Diagnostic signal dilution in complete traces.}\nBoth pathways' signals exist in full traces but are buried under thousands of intermediate tokens.\nFigure~\\ref{fig:training_dynamics} confirms: \\texttt{exception\\_only} converges within 50 steps, while \\texttt{full\\_trace} with label-only loss oscillates (82--89\\%) without convergence (Appendix~\\ref{sec:training_dynamics}); seed variance negatively correlates with diagnostic density.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{training_dynamics}\n\\caption{Training dynamics: \\texttt{exception\\_only} converges to 99.83\\% by step 150; \\texttt{full\\_trace} with label-only loss oscillates (82--89\\%) without convergence.}\n\\label{fig:training_dynamics}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\paragraph{Why SFT over regex?}\nSFT \\texttt{exception\\_only} (99.83\\%) exceeds the best regex (B\\_marker, 99.33\\%) by 0.50pp on 6 caught-exception boundary cases (McNemar $p{=}.014$), provides calibrated probabilities (ECE\\,=\\,0.0027; \\S\\ref{sec:calibration}), and retains 97.65\\% under marker removal vs.\\ B5's 28.49\\% (\\S\\ref{sec:nomarker}).\nThe residual 2/1190 errors (both \\texttt{GeneratorExit}; Appendix~\\ref{sec:irreducible}) are shared across seeds; CodeLlama-13B seed 123 achieves 100\\%.\n\n\\paragraph{The marginal value of complete traces.}\nSource code alone achieves 73--76\\% across four models (\\texttt{no\\_trace}), so the total incremental value of traces is at most ${\\sim}$27pp.\nComplete traces capture only 4--9pp of this headroom (77--82\\%), while exception events capture 24--27pp (${\\geq}$99.33\\%).\nThe finding that more information degrades performance, rather than the success of compact signals, is the primary contribution: it challenges the common assumption that richer execution context benefits SFT.\nThis aligns with the classical principle that irrelevant features degrade classifiers \\citep{guyon2003introduction}, the Information Bottleneck prediction that optimal representations compress task-irrelevant information \\citep{tishby2000information}, and LIMA's \\citep{zhou2023lima} demonstration that minimal high-quality data suffices for alignment.\n\n\\paragraph{Design implications.}\nThe two-tier hierarchy (Table~\\ref{tab:cross_model}) suggests a practical fallback: extract exception events first (explicit pathway); resort to tail-truncated execution state when no exception is raised (implicit pathway).\nTier~2 benefit is model-dependent (Qwen +10.9/+7.2pp at 7B/1.5B; CodeLlama +0.9pp), so trace design should match deployment model capacity.\nWhether this framework transfers to other trace-based tasks \\citep{gu2024cruxeval,ni2024next} remains open.\n\n\\paragraph{Caveats.}\nThe decomposition (\\S\\ref{sec:density}) is a preliminary upper-bound estimate on the Qwen family only (CodeLlama and DeepSeek exhibit label-only collapse); $N{=}4$ models is insufficient for a continuous density--accuracy relationship.\nHumanEval/MBPP covers only simple assert-based tests; real-world failure modes remain untested.\n",
    "userModified": false
  },

}

     - New Abstract:
       ```latex
       \begin{abstract}
       Complete execution traces contain all information needed for verdict prediction (classifying whether a test passes or fails), yet they actively impair SFT learning.
       Across four models spanning three architecture families (1.5B--13B parameters), complete traces achieve only 77--82\%, barely improving over source code alone (${\sim}$73--76\%) and trailing compact exception events (${\geq}$99.33\%) by 18+pp.
       A regex exploiting format markers matches the exception ceiling (99.33\%), but marker removal collapses it to 28.49\% while SFT retains 97.65\%; cross-dataset transfer (99.41\%) confirms SFT acquires genuine diagnostic pattern recognition beyond format matching.
       A preliminary decomposition (confounded upper-bound estimate on the Qwen family) attributes ${\geq}$74\% of the degradation to diagnostic signal dilution at inference, though the label-only ablation simultaneously alters gradient selection, regularization, and effective learning rate.
       A two-tier signal hierarchy holds across families, with a sharp performance transition at ${\sim}$512 tail tokens, yielding a practical trace selection strategy: extract exception events first (Tier~1, ${\geq}$98.53\%), fall back to tail-truncated state otherwise (93--97\%).
       For SFT-based verdict prediction, the binding constraint is diagnostic signal density, not information quantity.
       \end{abstract}
       ```
     - New Limitations:
       ```latex
       \section*{Limitations}
       Our study has several limitations.
       First, all experiments use HumanEval and MBPP-sanitized (591 problems, simple assert-based tests on short Python functions); generalization to human-written code, other languages, non-assert test frameworks (e.g., parametric tests, integration tests, flaky tests), and multi-file projects is unverified.
       Second, all four models (1.5B--13B) are code-specialized; models at 30B+ parameters may have sufficient capacity to overcome diagnostic signal dilution, and general-purpose LLMs may behave differently.
       Third, multi-seed experiments use only 3 seeds (the minimum for variance estimation); larger seed pools would strengthen robustness claims.
       Fourth, \texttt{full\_trace} was not evaluated on DebugBench due to the supplementary nature of that analysis; whether complete traces degrade on human-written buggy code remains untested.
       Fifth, the decomposition (\S\ref{sec:density}) is a confounded upper-bound estimate limited to the Qwen family; future work should isolate individual confounders through controlled gradient masking ablations.
       ```

        "content": "\\section{Discussion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\n\\paragraph{A descriptive two-pathway framework.}\nThe results suggest execution traces contain two independent signal pathways for verdict prediction, whose interaction explains why complete traces degrade SFT learning.\nWe present this as a descriptive working hypothesis, not a causal model.\n\n\\paragraph{Explicit pathway: diagnostic tokens.}\nThe diagnostic token ratio $d$ (\\S2.1) correlates with accuracy at $r{=}0.949$ ($p{=}0.014$) across five conditions, and all four models achieve ${\\geq}$99.33\\% under \\texttt{exception\\_only} with zero variance on Qwen ($\\sigma{=}$0.00, 3 seeds).\nAlthough 55.5\\% of failures are silent logic errors, \\texttt{exception\\_only} achieves 100\\% fail recall because test assertions generate \\texttt{AssertionError} events.\n\\texttt{ae\\_only}, a strict superset, slightly underperforms \\texttt{exception\\_only} (Table~\\ref{tab:ablation}), confirming that additional content introduces noise.\n\n\\paragraph{Implicit pathway: terminal execution state.}\nTail-truncated traces achieve 93--97\\% accuracy (Table~\\ref{tab:tail_cross_model}) despite $d {\\approx} 0$.\nTail truncation at ${\\geq}$512 tokens captures \\texttt{[Line~X]} variable values at final execution steps, which indirectly encode program outcome; below 384 tokens, the globals module dump consumes the entire budget, yielding ${\\sim}$73\\% ($\\approx$ \\texttt{no\\_trace}).\nThe +13.8pp phase transition at tail-384$\\to$tail-512 marks where the model accesses terminal execution state.\n\n\\paragraph{Diagnostic signal dilution in complete traces.}\nBoth pathways' signals exist in full traces but are buried under thousands of intermediate tokens.\nFigure~\\ref{fig:training_dynamics} confirms: \\texttt{exception\\_only} converges within 50 steps, while \\texttt{full\\_trace} with label-only loss oscillates (82--89\\%) without convergence (Appendix~\\ref{sec:training_dynamics}); seed variance negatively correlates with diagnostic density.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{training_dynamics}\n\\caption{Training dynamics: \\texttt{exception\\_only} converges to 99.83\\% by step 150; \\texttt{full\\_trace} with label-only loss oscillates (82--89\\%) without convergence.}\n\\label{fig:training_dynamics}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\paragraph{Why SFT over regex?}\nSFT \\texttt{exception\\_only} (99.83\\%) exceeds the best regex (B5, 99.33\\%) by 0.50pp on caught-exception boundary cases (McNemar $p{=}.014$), provides calibrated probabilities (ECE\\,=\\,0.0027; \\S\\ref{sec:calibration}), and retains 97.65\\% under marker removal vs.\\ B5's 28.49\\% (\\S\\ref{sec:nomarker}).\n\n\\paragraph{The marginal value of complete traces.}\nSource code alone achieves 73--76\\% (\\texttt{no\\_trace}), so traces add at most ${\\sim}$27pp.\nComplete traces capture only 4--9pp of this headroom (77--82\\%), while exception events capture 24--27pp (${\\geq}$99.33\\%).\nThat more information degrades performance is the primary finding; it aligns with classical feature selection theory \\citep{guyon2003introduction}, the Information Bottleneck \\citep{tishby2000information}, and LIMA's \\citep{zhou2023lima} demonstration that minimal high-quality data suffices.\n\n\\paragraph{Design implications.}\nThe two-tier hierarchy suggests a practical fallback: extract exception events first; resort to tail-truncated state when no exception is raised.\nTier~2 benefit is model-dependent (Qwen +10.9/+7.2pp; CodeLlama +0.9pp), so trace design should match deployment model capacity.\nWhether this framework transfers to other trace-based tasks \\citep{gu2024cruxeval,ni2024next} remains open.\n\n\\paragraph{Caveats.}\nThe decomposition (\\S\\ref{sec:density}) is a preliminary upper-bound estimate on the Qwen family only (CodeLlama and DeepSeek exhibit label-only collapse); $N{=}4$ models is insufficient for a continuous density--accuracy relationship.\nHumanEval/MBPP covers only simple assert-based tests; real-world failure modes remain untested.\n",
        "numLines": 45,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 45
      }
    },

{
  "parentUuid": "276b9302-b43c-487f-909e-a4c75388550e",
  "isSidechain": false,
  "attachment": {
    "type": "file",

    "content": {
      "type": "text",
      "file": {
        "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_4/projects/trace-verdict-study/docs/paper/related_work.tex",
        "content": "\\section{Related Work}\n\n\\paragraph{Execution Traces for Code LLMs.}\nTracePile \\citep{chen2025tracepile} scaled trace-augmented training to 2.6M samples; \\citet{wang2025dcsh} found trace utility task- and format-dependent, which we extend by showing that complete traces degrade SFT performance while compact exception events suffice.\nNExT \\citep{ni2024next} and ET-CoT \\citep{eguchi2025etcot} used trace chain-of-thought for repair and execution reasoning; \\citet{ferrando2025execute} found compact traces comparable to full traces, supporting our density finding.\n\\citet{liu2023codeexecution} pre-trained on execution traces; CRUXEval \\citep{gu2024cruxeval,xu2025cruxevalx} established execution reasoning benchmarks.\nNone decompose which trace components contribute to \\emph{classification} accuracy.\n\n\\paragraph{Code Verdict and Patch Classification.}\nTRACED \\citep{ding2024traced} showed execution-aware pretraining improves vulnerability detection; CC2Vec \\citep{hoang2020cc2vec}, LLM4PatchCorrect \\citep{zhou2024llm4patch}, and APPT \\citep{appt2024} developed representation learning of code changes.\nVerdict prediction is an instance of the test oracle problem \\citep{barr2015oracle}; none isolate individual trace components.\n\n\\paragraph{Fault Localization and Trace Selection.}\nSBFL \\citep{abreu2007accuracy,jones2002visualization,wong2014dstar} and LLM-based FL \\citep{kang2024autofl,qin2024agentfl} share our insight that anomalous execution events carry the strongest diagnostic signal.\nTraceCoder \\citep{huang2026tracecoder} and InspectCoder \\citep{wang2025inspectcoder} use selective trace extraction; RepairAgent \\citep{bouzenia2025repairagent}, Agentless \\citep{xia2024agentless}, SWE-agent \\citep{yang2024sweagent}, and OpenHands \\citep{wang2025openhands} incorporate execution feedback in repair workflows \\citep{jimenez2024swebench}, yet which trace components carry verdict signals most efficiently remains unstudied.\nPrompt compression \\citep{jiang2023llmlingua,li2023selectivecontext} showed pruning low-information tokens preserves accuracy; our decomposition quantifies \\emph{why}.\n\n\\paragraph{Calibration, Data Selection, and Defect Prediction.}\nNeural network calibration \\citep{guo2017calibration}, extended to LLMs \\citep{kadavath2022language,geng2024calibration,spiess2025calibration}, studies confidence-correctness alignment; our ECE ${<}$ 0.005 suggests compact inputs improve calibration.\nThe Information Bottleneck \\citep{tishby2000information}, feature selection theory \\citep{guyon2003introduction}, and LIMA \\citep{zhou2023lima} predict that compressing task-irrelevant information improves learning; data pruning \\citep{paul2021deep,sorscher2022beyond} and curriculum learning \\citep{bengio2009curriculum} confirm this empirically, consistent with our finding that compact exceptions converge rapidly while verbose traces oscillate.\nJust-in-time defect prediction \\citep{kamei2013large} shares our fault detection motivation.\n",
        "numLines": 22,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 22
      }
    },
    "displayPath": "../../../docs/paper/related_work.tex"
  },

      "file": {
        "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_4/projects/trace-verdict-study/docs/paper/conclusion.tex",
        "content": "\\section{Conclusion}\n\nAcross four models (1.5B--13B, three families), complete execution traces (77--82\\%) barely improve over source code alone (${\\sim}$73--76\\%) while compact exception events achieve ${\\geq}$99.33\\%; cross-dataset transfer (99.41\\%) and marker-removal robustness (97.65\\% vs.\\ regex collapse to 28.49\\%) confirm SFT learns genuine diagnostic patterns.\nA two-tier signal hierarchy yields a practical trace selection strategy: extract exception events first, fall back to tail-truncated state (93--97\\%) otherwise.\nA preliminary decomposition attributes ${\\geq}$74\\% of the degradation to diagnostic signal dilution at inference (confounded upper-bound estimate); controlled gradient masking ablations remain the critical next step.\nThese findings suggest that trace-based tool design should prioritize diagnostic density over completeness.\nGeneralization to human-written code, other languages, non-assert tests, and larger models remains open.\n",
        "numLines": 8,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 8
      }

```json
{
  "parentUuid": "b4531d09-2c48-483e-bdc1-3036cca67a35",
  "isSidechain": false,
  "attachment": {
    "type": "file",
    "filename": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_4/projects/trace-verdict-study/docs/paper/introduction.tex",
    "content": {
      "type": "text",
      "file": {
        "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_4/projects/trace-verdict-study/docs/paper/introduction.tex",
        "content": "\\section{Introduction}\n\nExecution traces (line-by-line records of a program's runtime behavior) have emerged as a promising input modality for code language models \\citep{chen2025tracepile,ding2024traced,wang2025dcsh,ni2024next}.\nThe intuition is straightforward: more execution information should help the model make better predictions.\nYet for verdict prediction (classifying whether a test passes or fails), this intuition is wrong.\nComplete traces \\emph{actively degrade} accuracy compared to compact exception events, despite containing strictly more information.\n\nPrior work found trace utility to be task-dependent \\citep{wang2025dcsh}, but focused on generation-oriented tasks where output noise confounds the analysis.\nVerdict prediction offers a cleaner testbed: as binary classification, it isolates the learning signal from generation noise, and controlled component ablation directly measures how trace content affects SFT learning.\nThis approach aligns with recent *CL precedent: CRUXEval \\citep{gu2024cruxeval} uses execution prediction to probe code reasoning, and NExT \\citep{ni2024next} uses trace-augmented chain-of-thought for program repair.\n\nWe present a systematic component ablation of trace representations for verdict prediction.\nUsing 591 problems from HumanEval \\citep{chen2021evaluating} and MBPP-sanitized \\citep{austin2021program}, we evaluate four models across three architecture families and 1.5B to 13B parameters: Qwen-2.5-Coder-7B/1.5B-Instruct \\citep{qwen2024coder}, CodeLlama-13B-Instruct \\citep{roziere2024code}, and DeepSeek-Coder-6.7B-Instruct \\citep{deepseek2024coder}.\n\nThe central result: complete traces (77--82\\%) barely improve over source code alone (${\\sim}$73--76\\%) while exception events achieve ${\\geq}$99.33\\% across all models and seeds (\\S\\ref{sec:cross_model}).\nA simple regex baseline (B5) that exploits the \\texttt{[No exception data in trace]} format marker matches this ceiling at 99.33\\%; even a trivial pattern suffices, so the interesting question is not why exceptions help but \\emph{why complete traces hurt}.\nMarker removal collapses B5 to 28.49\\% while SFT retains 97.65\\% ($-$2.01pp); cross-dataset transfer at 99.41\\% (HumanEval${\\to}$MBPP) confirms that SFT acquires genuine diagnostic patterns beyond format matching (\\S\\ref{sec:nomarker}).\n\nA preliminary decomposition on the Qwen family (7B and 1.5B, 3 seeds each) provides a confounded upper-bound estimate: gradient dilution accounts for ${\\leq}$26\\% of the gap, with diagnostic signal dilution (the mechanism underlying what is intuitively termed ``information overload'') at ${\\geq}$74\\%, though the label-only ablation simultaneously alters gradient selection, regularization, and effective learning rate (\\S\\ref{sec:density}).\nTraining dynamics confirm this: \\texttt{exception\\_only} converges within 50 steps, while \\texttt{full\\_trace} oscillates between 82--89\\% without stable convergence (\\S\\ref{sec:ablation}, Appendix~\\ref{sec:training_dynamics}).\nA tail-truncation budget sweep reveals a sharp performance jump at ${\\sim}$512 tokens; this threshold coincides with the globals module dump (${\\sim}$384 tokens), beyond which structured \\texttt{[Line~X]} variable values appear (\\S\\ref{sec:trace_length}).\n\nOur contributions:\n\\begin{enumerate}\n\\item \\textbf{Complete traces are strictly dominated by compact exception events}: across four models (1.5B--13B, three families), complete traces (77--82\\%) barely improve over source code alone (${\\sim}$73--76\\%) while exception events achieve ${\\geq}$99.33\\% (\\S\\ref{sec:cross_model}). Training dynamics directly show the mechanism: \\texttt{exception\\_only} converges within 50 steps while \\texttt{full\\_trace} oscillates without stable convergence (\\S\\ref{sec:ablation}, Appendix~\\ref{sec:training_dynamics}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{SFT learns genuine diagnostic patterns beyond format matching}: a regex exploiting format markers matches the exception ceiling (99.33\\%), but marker removal collapses it to 28.49\\% while SFT retains 97.65\\% ($-$2.01pp); cross-dataset transfer at 99.41\\% confirms the learned signal generalizes (\\S\\ref{sec:nomarker}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{A two-tier signal hierarchy with a practical trace selection strategy}: Tier~1 (exceptions, assertions; ${\\geq}$98.53\\%) dominates Tier~2 (loop compression; 77--86\\%) by 12+pp with non-overlapping CIs across all four models. A sharp performance transition at ${\\sim}$512 tail tokens yields a practical fallback: exception events first, tail-truncated state otherwise (\\S\\ref{sec:ablation}, \\S\\ref{sec:trace_length}).\n\n\\item \\textbf{Preliminary decomposition of the full-trace degradation}: a confounded upper-bound estimate via label-only loss on the Qwen family (7B and 1.5B, 3 seeds each) attributes gradient dilution ${\\leq}$26\\% and diagnostic signal dilution ${\\geq}$74\\% of the gap, though the ablation simultaneously alters gradient selection, regularization, and effective learning rate (\\S\\ref{sec:density}).\n\\end{enumerate}\n",
        "numLines": 33,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 33
      }
    },
    "displayPath": "../../../docs/paper/introduction.tex"
  },

```

The two-tier approach prioritizes exception events as the primary signal, with tail-truncated state as a fallback when exceptions aren't available. About three-quarters of the performance drop seems to stem from diagnostic signal dilution during inference, though this needs more rigorous ablation work to confirm. The broader takeaway is that trace-based debugging tools should focus on signal quality rather than trying to capture everything. There are still open questions around how well this generalizes to human-written code, other programming languages, different test types, and larger model scales.

For the discussion section structure, I'm planning eight main points: a two-pathway framework explaining how traces work, the explicit pathway through diagnostic tokens, the implicit pathway via terminal state, why complete traces dilute the signal, justification for SFT over regex matching, the marginal returns of comprehensive traces, design implications for tool builders, and important caveats about the scope of these findings.

The third point describes a hierarchical signal structure where exceptions and assertions form the dominant tier, with loop compression and other signals in a secondary tier.

    "originalFile": "\\section{Conclusion}\n\nAcross four models (1.5B--13B, three families), complete execution traces (77--82\\%) barely improve over source code alone (${\\sim}$73--76\\%) while compact exception events achieve ${\\geq}$99.33\\%; cross-dataset transfer (99.41\\%) and marker-removal robustness (97.65\\% vs.\\ regex collapse to 28.49\\%) confirm SFT learns genuine diagnostic patterns.\nA two-tier signal hierarchy yields a practical trace selection strategy: extract exception events first, fall back to tail-truncated state (93--97\\%) otherwise.\nA preliminary decomposition attributes ${\\geq}$74\\% of the degradation to diagnostic signal dilution at inference (confounded upper-bound estimate); controlled gradient masking ablations remain the critical next step.\nThese findings suggest that trace-based tool design should prioritize diagnostic density over completeness.\nGeneralization to human-written code, other languages, non-assert tests, and larger models remains open.\n",

{
  "recorded_role": "user",

  "toolUseResult": {
    "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_4/projects/trace-verdict-study/docs/paper/discussion.tex",
    "oldString": "\\paragraph{Caveats.}\nThe decomposition (\\S\\ref{sec:density}) is a preliminary upper-bound estimate on the Qwen family only (CodeLlama and DeepSeek exhibit label-only collapse); $N{=}4$ models is insufficient for a continuous density--accuracy relationship.\nHumanEval/MBPP covers only simple assert-based tests; real-world failure modes remain untested.",
    "newString": "",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Discussion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\n\\paragraph{A descriptive two-pathway framework.}\nThe results suggest execution traces contain two independent signal pathways for verdict prediction, whose interaction explains why complete traces degrade SFT learning.\nWe present this as a descriptive working hypothesis, not a causal model.\n\n\\paragraph{Explicit pathway: diagnostic tokens.}\nThe diagnostic token ratio $d$ (\\S2.1) correlates with accuracy at $r{=}0.949$ ($p{=}0.014$) across five conditions, and all four models achieve ${\\geq}$99.33\\% under \\texttt{exception\\_only} with zero variance on Qwen ($\\sigma{=}$0.00, 3 seeds).\nAlthough 55.5\\% of failures are silent logic errors, \\texttt{exception\\_only} achieves 100\\% fail recall because test assertions generate \\texttt{AssertionError} events.\n\\texttt{ae\\_only}, a strict superset, slightly underperforms \\texttt{exception\\_only} (Table~\\ref{tab:ablation}), confirming that additional content introduces noise.\n\n\\paragraph{Implicit pathway: terminal execution state.}\nTail-truncated traces achieve 93--97\\% accuracy (Table~\\ref{tab:tail_cross_model}) despite $d {\\approx} 0$.\nTail truncation at ${\\geq}$512 tokens captures \\texttt{[Line~X]} variable values at final execution steps, which indirectly encode program outcome; below 384 tokens, the globals module dump consumes the entire budget, yielding ${\\sim}$73\\% ($\\approx$ \\texttt{no\\_trace}).\nThe +13.8pp phase transition at tail-384$\\to$tail-512 marks where the model accesses terminal execution state.\n\n\\paragraph{Diagnostic signal dilution in complete traces.}\nBoth pathways' signals exist in full traces but are buried under thousands of intermediate tokens.\nFigure~\\ref{fig:training_dynamics} confirms: \\texttt{exception\\_only} converges within 50 steps, while \\texttt{full\\_trace} with label-only loss oscillates (82--89\\%) without convergence (Appendix~\\ref{sec:training_dynamics}); seed variance negatively correlates with diagnostic density.\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{training_dynamics}\n\\caption{Training dynamics: \\texttt{exception\\_only} converges to 99.83\\% by step 150; \\texttt{full\\_trace} with label-only loss oscillates (82--89\\%) without convergence.}\n\\label{fig:training_dynamics}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\paragraph{Why SFT over regex?}\nSFT \\texttt{exception\\_only} (99.83\\%) exceeds the best regex (B5, 99.33\\%) by 0.50pp on caught-exception boundary cases (McNemar $p{=}.014$), provides calibrated probabilities (ECE\\,=\\,0.0027; \\S\\ref{sec:calibration}), and retains 97.65\\% under marker removal vs.\\ B5's 28.49\\% (\\S\\ref{sec:nomarker}).\n\n\\paragraph{The marginal value of complete traces.}\nSource code alone achieves 73--76\\% (\\texttt{no\\_trace}), so traces add at most ${\\sim}$27pp.\nComplete traces capture only 4--9pp of this headroom (77--82\\%), while exception events capture 24--27pp (${\\geq}$99.33\\%).\nThat more information degrades performance is the primary finding; it aligns with classical feature selection theory \\citep{guyon2003introduction}, the Information Bottleneck \\citep{tishby2000information}, and LIMA's \\citep{zhou2023lima} demonstration that minimal high-quality data suffices.\n\n\\paragraph{Design implications.}\nThe two-tier hierarchy suggests a practical fallback: extract exception events first; resort to tail-truncated state when no exception is raised.\nTier~2 benefit is model-dependent (Qwen +10.9/+7.2pp; CodeLlama +0.9pp), so trace design should match deployment model capacity.\nWhether this framework transfers to other trace-based tasks \\citep{gu2024cruxeval,ni2024next} remains open.\n\n\\paragraph{Caveats.}\nThe decomposition (\\S\\ref{sec:density}) is a preliminary upper-bound estimate on the Qwen family only (CodeLlama and DeepSeek exhibit label-only collapse); $N{=}4$ models is insufficient for a continuous density--accuracy relationship.\nHumanEval/MBPP covers only simple assert-based tests; real-world failure modes remain untested.\n",
    "structuredPatch": [

        "oldLines": 6,

          " Whether this framework transfers to other trace-based tasks \\citep{gu2024cruxeval,ni2024next} remains open.",

[Director 指令] **[Patch] Reviewer 审查 Introduction/Conclusion — 3 处修正 + Conclusion 同步**

Abstract 优秀 ✅。以下是 Introduction 的 3 处问题和 Conclusion 的同步需求：

### Introduction 修正（3 处）

**1. Contribution 1 (line ~25): "eliminated" → "reduced"**
当前: "even when gradient dilution is eliminated"
问题: Abstract 同一页说 "simultaneously alters gradient selection, regularization, and effective learning rate"，用 "eliminated" 直接矛盾 MF-2 降级要求。
改为: "even when gradient dilution is **reduced**"

**2. Contribution 2 (line ~26): "~91% semantic" 改为更直接表述**
当前: "~91% semantic"
问题: 91.2% = (97.65-75.13)/(99.83-75.13) 但读者无法推导。
改为: "retains 97.65% accuracy under marker removal (vs. regex collapse to 28.49%, §3.3)"

**3. (Optional) Intro body 补充 B5 一句**
如果有空间，在 "central result" 段末尾加一句：
"A regex exploiting format markers already achieves 99.33% (§3.3), so we focus on **why** complete traces degrade rather than **whether** exceptions help."

### Conclusion 同步 reframe（必须）

Conclusion 未参与 Phase 1 重写，仍使用旧语言：
- "a decomposition attributes ≥74% of the gap to information overload" → 加 "confounded upper-bound estimate" + list confounders
- "A two-pathway model" → "descriptive framework" / "working hypothesis"
- **新增**：提及 B5/nomarker/cross-dataset，与新 Abstract 对齐
- **新增**：broader implications (1-2 句) + next step (controlled gradient masking)

控制在 page 8 以内。

```text
1	\section{Conclusion}
2	
3	Across four models (1.5B--13B, three families), complete execution traces (77--82\%) barely improve over source code alone (${\sim}$73--76\%) while compact exception events achieve ${\geq}$99.33\%; cross-dataset transfer (99.41\%) and marker-removal robustness (97.65\% vs.\ regex collapse to 28.49\%) confirm SFT learns genuine diagnostic patterns.
4	A two-tier signal hierarchy yields a practical trace selection strategy: extract exception events first, fall back to tail-truncated state (93--97\%) otherwise.
5	A preliminary decomposition attributes ${\geq}$74\% of the degradation to diagnostic signal dilution at inference (confounded upper-bound estimate); controlled gradient masking ablations remain the critical next step.
6	These findings suggest that trace-based tool design should prioritize diagnostic density over completeness; generalization to human-written code, other languages, non-assert tests, and larger models remains open.
7	
```

### Tool result

_source line 7515 · 2026-05-22T06:53:39.983Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01PBpcp9BkUFChMfqrGJ8nVP`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Introduction}
2	
3	Execution traces (line-by-line records of a program's runtime behavior) have emerged as a promising input modality for code language models \citep{chen2025tracepile,ding2024traced,wang2025dcsh,ni2024next}.
4	The intuition is straightforward: more execution information should help the model make better predictions.
5	Yet for verdict prediction (classifying whether a test passes or fails), this intuition is wrong.
6	Complete traces \emph{actively degrade} accuracy compared to compact exception events, despite containing strictly more information.
7	
8	Prior work found trace utility to be task-dependent \citep{wang2025dcsh}, but focused on generation-oriented tasks where output noise confounds the analysis.
9	Verdict prediction offers a cleaner testbed: as binary classification, it isolates the learning signal from generation noise, and controlled component ablation directly measures how trace content affects SFT learning.
10	This approach aligns with recent *CL precedent: CRUXEval \citep{gu2024cruxeval} uses execution prediction to probe code reasoning, and NExT \citep{ni2024next} uses trace-augmented chain-of-thought for program repair.
11	
12	We present a systematic component ablation of trace representations for verdict prediction.
13	Using 591 problems from HumanEval \citep{chen2021evaluating} and MBPP-sanitized \citep{austin2021program}, we evaluate four models across three architecture families and 1.5B to 13B parameters: Qwen-2.5-Coder-7B/1.5B-Instruct \citep{qwen2024coder}, CodeLlama-13B-Instruct \citep{roziere2024code}, and DeepSeek-Coder-6.7B-Instruct \citep{deepseek2024coder}.
14	
15	The central result: complete traces (77--82\%) barely improve over source code alone (${\sim}$73--76\%) while exception events achieve ${\geq}$99.33\% across all models and seeds (\S\ref{sec:cross_model}).
16	A simple regex baseline (B5) that exploits the \texttt{[No exception data in trace]} format marker matches this ceiling at 99.33\%; even a trivial pattern suffices, so the interesting question is not why exceptions help but \emph{why complete traces hurt}.
17	Marker removal collapses B5 to 28.49\% while SFT retains 97.65\% ($-$2.01pp); cross-dataset transfer at 99.41\% (HumanEval${\to}$MBPP) confirms that SFT acquires genuine diagnostic patterns beyond format matching (\S\ref{sec:nomarker}).
18	
19	A preliminary decomposition on the Qwen family (7B and 1.5B, 3 seeds each) provides a confounded upper-bound estimate: gradient dilution accounts for ${\leq}$26\% of the gap, with diagnostic signal dilution (the mechanism underlying what is intuitively termed ``information overload'') at ${\geq}$74\%, though the label-only ablation simultaneously alters gradient selection, regularization, and effective learning rate (\S\ref{sec:density}).
20	Training dynamics confirm this: \texttt{exception\_only} converges within 50 steps, while \texttt{full\_trace} oscillates between 82--89\% without stable convergence (\S\ref{sec:ablation}, Appendix~\ref{sec:training_dynamics}).
21	A tail-truncation budget sweep reveals a sharp performance jump at ${\sim}$512 tokens; this threshold coincides with the globals module dump (${\sim}$384 tokens), beyond which structured \texttt{[Line~X]} variable values appear (\S\ref{sec:trace_length}).
22	
23	Our contributions:
24	\begin{enumerate}
25	\item \textbf{Complete traces are strictly dominated by compact exception events}: across four models (1.5B--13B, three families), complete traces (77--82\%) barely improve over source code alone (${\sim}$73--76\%) while exception events achieve ${\geq}$99.33\% (\S\ref{sec:cross_model}). Training dynamics directly show the mechanism: \texttt{exception\_only} converges within 50 steps while \texttt{full\_trace} oscillates without stable convergence (\S\ref{sec:ablation}, Appendix~\ref{sec:training_dynamics}).
26	
27	\item \textbf{SFT learns genuine diagnostic patterns beyond format matching}: a regex exploiting format markers matches the exception ceiling (99.33\%), but marker removal collapses it to 28.49\% while SFT retains 97.65\% ($-$2.01pp); cross-dataset transfer at 99.41\% confirms the learned signal generalizes (\S\ref{sec:nomarker}).
28	
29	\item \textbf{A two-tier signal hierarchy with a practical trace selection strategy}: Tier~1 (exceptions, assertions; ${\geq}$98.53\%) dominates Tier~2 (loop compression; 77--86\%) by 12+pp with non-overlapping CIs across all four models. A sharp performance transition at ${\sim}$512 tail tokens yields a practical fallback: exception events first, tail-truncated state otherwise (\S\ref{sec:ablation}, \S\ref{sec:trace_length}).
30	
31	\item \textbf{Preliminary decomposition of the full-trace degradation}: a confounded upper-bound estimate via label-only loss on the Qwen family (7B and 1.5B, 3 seeds each) attributes gradient dilution ${\leq}$26\% and diagnostic signal dilution ${\geq}$74\% of the gap, though the ablation simultaneously alters gradient selection, regularization, and effective learning rate (\S\ref{sec:density}).
32	\end{enumerate}
33	
```

[Director 指令] **[Phase 2 数据 — 部分] 统计实验结果出炉，影响 §3.4 写法**

### MF-3 Holm-Bonferroni — 好消息
- 80 个 pairwise 比较中，校正后仅 3 个失去显著性（全部是同 tier 内 borderline）
- **所有核心 claims 不受影响**：full_trace vs no_trace 显著，tier 结构稳健
- 论文：Table 1 脚注或 appendix 报告 "Holm-Bonferroni corrected p-values; 3/80 borderline within-tier comparisons lost significance"
- 失去显著性的 3 个：Qwen-1.5B ae_only vs exception_only (0.75pp), Qwen-1.5B ae_only vs nomarker (1.28pp), DeepSeek loop_only vs vota (3.19pp)

### MF-4 Clustered Bootstrap — 重要更新
- **effective n=119（非 607！）**：每个 HumanEval 问题恰好 10 个变体，duplication_rate=90%
- CI 宽度增加 2.24x（clustered vs original）
- 14/19 核心比较仍显著，tier 结构 + "traces hurt" 安全
- **⚠️ 关键：label_only vs full_trace 不再显著**（Qwen-7B: 4.45pp, clustered CI=[-0.59, +9.75]，跨零）
- **论文影响**：§3.4 decomposition 的 "≤74% inference-time" claim 失去统计支撑。写法必须进一步降级为 "a numerical trend suggesting ≤74%... however, this difference does not reach significance under problem-level clustered bootstrap (95% CI [-0.6, +9.8]pp)"
- 报告 effective n=119 + clustered CIs 作为 primary

### SF-5 Tier Clustering — 好消息
- Gap statistic 支持 k=2（3/4 模型 + pooled 分析）
- Silhouette 0.747-0.967，统计支撑强
- **可以保留 "two-tier hierarchy" 语言**，不需要降级为 "observed pattern"
- 例外：DeepSeek vota borderline（可能数据质量问题），CodeLlama loop_only ≈ no_trace（模型特异性）
- 论文：增加一句 "gap statistic supports k=2 for 3/4 models (silhouette ≥ 0.74)"

### 写法总结
1. §3.4 decomposition 进一步降级：label_only vs full_trace 不显著 → "numerical trend, not statistically confirmed"
2. Table 1：报告 clustered CIs (n_eff=119) 作为 primary
3. Two-tier hierarchy：可用 k-means/gap statistic 统计支撑
4. Holm-Bonferroni：报告 adjusted p-values，核心 claims 全部 survive
